<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a bounded, continuous, stable radial pair potential in three dimensions with $`|\phi(r)|\le Cr^{-3-1/32}`$ for <i>r</i> ≥ 1. Throughout an open interval of positive densities, its canonical thermodynamic free energy is finite at every positive inverse temperature and has a strict downward derivative jump at one common finite positive inverse temperature.

- **C-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a bounded continuous function $\phi:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb R$ and finite constants $B,C>0$ such that $$\sum_{i<j}\phi(\left\|x_i-x_j\right\|)\ge-BN, \qquad \left\|\phi(r)\right\|\le C r^{-3-1/32}\quad(r\ge1).$$ There are an open interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$ centered at $5p/3$ and a common inverse temperature $\beta_c\in[7/8,9/8]$ such that, for every $\rho\in I$, the canonical free energy $$f(\beta,\rho)= -\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac1{\beta L^3} \log Z^\phi_{L,\lfloor\rho L^3\rfloor}(\beta)$$ exists and is finite for every $\beta>0$. Its finite one-sided derivatives at $\beta_c$ satisfy $$f'_-(\beta_c,\rho)>f'_+(\beta_c,\rho).$$ In particular, $f(\,\cdot\,,\rho)$ is not differentiable at $\beta_c$.
  - Explanation: Constructs a fixed bounded continuous stable radial pair potential whose canonical free energy has a common inverse-temperature derivative jump throughout an open density interval.
- **C-f-packing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-f-packing)): Let $M_L$ be the maximum cardinality of a subset of $[0,L]^3$ whose distinct points have mutual distances at least one. Then $$p=\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{M_L}{L^3}$$ exists and belongs to $(0,\infty)$.
  - Explanation: Establishes existence and positivity of the asymptotic density of unit-separated configurations.
- **C-f-shell-graph** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-shell-graph)): For any finite configuration in $\mathbb R^3$, join two particles when their distance belongs to $I_t$. This graph has no clique of size five. On any $m$ vertices it has at most $3m^2/8$ edges.
  - Explanation: Excludes shell cliques of size five using a Gram-matrix rank obstruction and bounds edges by weighted clique maximization.
- **C-f-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-stability)): Set $$\label{f:stability-constants} D_0=t^2/8,\qquad B_0=t^2/2.$$ For every configuration, including coincident particles, $$\label{f:cell-stability} U^{\psi_t}\ge D_0\sum_i n_i^2-B_0N, \qquad U^v\ge(D_0-M/2)\sum_i n_i^2-B_0N.$$ Choose $t$ sufficiently large that $$\label{f:t-requirements} D_0\ge8M,\qquad ct>4(A+1),\qquad c=1/3456.$$ Then, in any axis-parallel cube of side $L\ge1$, with $V=L^3$, $$\label{f:density-stability} U^v\ge ct^2\frac{N^2}{V}-\frac{t^2}{2}N.$$ In particular all members of <a href="#f:family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:family">[f:family]</a> are stable with the same constant $B_0$.
  - Explanation: Converts the shell graph estimate and the attraction row bound into uniform cell and density coercivity, including coincident particles.
- **C-f-canonical-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-canonical-bounds)): There is an absolute constant $C<\infty$, independent of $t$ and all attraction ranges, such that for every admissible $t$, $L\ge1$, $n\ge0$, $\beta>0$, and $d_L=n/L^3$, $$\label{f:finite-bounds} \beta t(cd_L^2-d_L/2)+\frac{d_L\log d_L-d_L}{t} \le H^\varphi_{L,n}(\beta) \le \beta Ct d_L^2+\frac{d_L\log d_L}{t},$$ where $0\log0=0$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the finite-volume normalized canonical free energy from stability and a uniform-position expectation estimate.
- **C-f-grand-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-grand-tail)): Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact $\mathcal D\subset\Omega$, where $\Omega=(0,\infty)\times\mathbb R$ has coordinates $(\beta,h)$. There are constants $D\ge1$ and $a_0>0$, depending only on $t$ and $\mathcal D$, such that, for every $\varphi$ in <a href="#f:family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:family">[f:family]</a>, $L\ge1$, and $0\le\zeta\le2(A+1)$, $$\label{f:grand-tail-bound} \sum_{n>DL^3}\mathrm e^{thn}Z^\varphi_{L,n}(\beta) \exp\left(\frac{\beta t\zeta n^2}{2L^3}\right) \le\frac{\exp[-a_0(\lfloor DL^3\rfloor+1)]}{1-\mathrm e^{-a_0}} \qquad ((\beta,h)\in\mathcal D).$$ In particular the tail tends uniformly to zero, and is at most one for all sufficiently large $L$. These constants require no local occupation estimate.
  - Explanation: Controls the large-particle-number grand sum uniformly on compact parameter sets using a coercive quadratic majorant.
- **C-f-thermodynamics** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-f-thermodynamics)): For every member $\varphi$ of <a href="#f:family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:family">[f:family]</a>, including countably infinite sums, and every $\beta>0$, $d\ge0$, the limit $$\label{f:canonical-limit} H_\varphi(\beta,d) =\lim_{\substack{L\to\infty\\ n/L^3\to d}} H^\varphi_{L,n}(\beta)$$ exists along every such sequence of real sides and integer counts. It is finite, convex and continuous in $d\in[0,\infty)$, and concave in $\beta>0$ for each fixed $d$. The bounds <a href="#f:finite-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:finite-bounds">[f:finite-bounds]</a> hold with $H_\varphi(\beta,d)$ and $d$ in place of their finite volume counterparts.* *The grand partition function and pressure satisfy $$\label{f:pressure-definition} \Xi^\varphi_L(\beta,h) =\sum_{n\ge0}\mathrm e^{thn}Z^\varphi_{L,n}(\beta), \qquad Q_\varphi(\beta,h) =\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{\log\Xi^\varphi_L(\beta,h)}{tL^3}.$$ The pressure is finite, nonnegative, and jointly convex on $\Omega$. For $\beta>0$, $$\label{f:duality} \begin{split} Q_\varphi(\beta,h) &=\sup_{d\ge0}\{hd-H_\varphi(\beta,d)\},\\ H_\varphi(\beta,d) &=\sup_{h\in\mathbb R}\{hd-Q_\varphi(\beta,h)\},\qquad d>0. \end{split}$$ For fixed admissible $t$, pressures of the whole family are uniformly bounded on each compact subset of $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: Constructs canonical and pressure limits for the entire family, including infinite attraction sums, and proves density-field duality.
- **C-f-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-moments)): Fix $t$ satisfying <a href="#f:t-requirements" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:t-requirements">[f:t-requirements]</a> and a compact $\mathcal D\subset\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\exp},C_1<\infty$, depending only on $t$ and $\mathcal D$, such that every grand canonical measure for an interaction <a href="#f:modified-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:modified-energy">[f:modified-energy]</a> satisfies $$\label{f:moment-estimates} \sup_i\mathbb E\exp(\beta D_0n_i^2/4)\le C_{\exp}, \qquad \sup_{i,j}\mathbb E[n_i n_j]\le C_1 \qquad ((\beta,h)\in\mathcal D).$$ The bounds hold for every bounded region with finitely many cropped cells, and uniformly for all permitted deletions and interpolations.
  - Explanation: Bounds local occupation moments uniformly in volume by a conditional energy estimate and a sublinear recursion.
- **C-f-perturbation** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-perturbation)): In a fixed bounded region, let $v_\theta=(1-\theta)v_0+\theta v_1$, $0\le\theta\le1$, be bounded symmetric pair interactions satisfying the hypotheses of [f:moments](#f:moments) with the same $t$ and $M$. Let $J^{\mathrm{diff}}$ be a symmetric nonnegative matrix such that $$\left\|v_1(x,y)-v_0(x,y)\right\|\le J^{\mathrm{diff}}_{ij} \quad (x\in C_i,\ y\in C_j).$$ Then, uniformly for $(\beta,h)\in\mathcal D$, $$\label{f:interpolation-bound} \left\|\log\Xi^{v_1}(\beta,h)-\log\Xi^{v_0}(\beta,h)\right\| \le\frac{\beta C_1}{2}\sum_{i,j}J^{\mathrm{diff}}_{ij}.$$ Thus signed deletions are controlled by the absolute interaction difference, with no cancellation assumption.
  - Explanation: Controls signed changes in the grand partition function by the absolute interaction difference without cancellation.
- **C-f-tail-pressure** (bound, [source](#stmt-f-tail-pressure)): For a finite or countably infinite member of <a href="#f:family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:family">[f:family]</a>, let $$\varphi_k=\psi_t-t\sum_{j\le k}\alpha_jw_{R_j}, \qquad m_k=\sum_{j>k}\alpha_j.$$ For every compact $\mathcal D\subset\Omega$ there is a constant $C_{\mathcal D,t}<\infty$, independent of the ranges, their number, and $k$, such that $$\label{f:tail-pressure-bound} 0\le Q_\varphi(\beta,h)-Q_{\varphi_k}(\beta,h) \le C_{\mathcal D,t}m_k \qquad ((\beta,h)\in\mathcal D).$$ The same upper bound holds for the difference of normalized finite volume log grand partition functions in every cube of side $L\ge1$.
  - Explanation: Controls the pressure effect of all omitted attractions by their total mass independently of their ranges.
- **C-k-transform-properties** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-k-transform-properties)): Suppose the size conditions on $t$ in [f:stability](#f:stability) hold, including $ct>4(A+1)$. For every $0<a\le 2(A+1)$, $$T_aQ(\beta,h) =\max_{d\ge0} \left\{hd-H(\beta,d)+\frac{\beta a}{2}d^2\right\}. \label{k:density-transform}$$ The active shifts in <a href="#k:transform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="k:transform">[k:transform]</a> are exactly the maximizing densities in <a href="#k:density-transform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="k:density-transform">[k:density-transform]</a>; in particular, they are nonnegative and form a nonempty compact set. On each compact $\mathcal D\subset\Omega$, they lie in a common interval $[0,U]$, independently of $\varphi$ and $a$. The constant $U$ may also be chosen uniformly for all sufficiently large $t$.* *The function $T_aQ$ is finite and convex on $\Omega$, and $$0\le T_aQ-Q\le C_{\mathcal D}a \qquad\hbox{on }\mathcal D. \label{k:small-mass}$$ Here $C_{\mathcal D}$ can be chosen uniformly for sufficiently large $t$. For fixed $t$, the pressures $Q$ and transforms $T_aQ$ have common bounds on compact subsets of $\Omega$, and common local Lipschitz bounds, uniformly over the same family and masses.
  - Explanation: Identifies active shifts with maximizing densities, proves attainment, and supplies uniform bounds for small attraction masses.
- **C-k-active-parabola** (bound, [source](#stmt-k-active-parabola)): Let $a>0$ be as in [k:transform-properties](#k:transform-properties), and let $u$ be active at $\lambda=(\beta,h)$. Then for every $r\in\mathbb R$, $$Q(x_u+(0,r))\le Q(x_u)+ur+\frac{r^2}{2\beta a}. \label{k:parabola}$$ Every member of $\partial Q(x_u)$ has second coordinate $u$.
  - Explanation: Shows that an active shifted point has a parabolic upper bound in the field coordinate and a uniquely specified density coordinate for all supports.
- **C-k-subdifferential** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-k-subdifferential)): For each fixed $0<a\le2(A+1)$ and $\lambda=(\beta,h)\in\Omega$, $$\partial(T_aQ)(\lambda) =\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\!\bigcup_{u\text{ active at }\lambda} \left\{\left(e+\frac a2u^2,u\right):(e,u)\in\partial Q(x_u)\right\}. \label{k:support-hull}$$ The union on the right is compact, and every one of its members is an actual supporting slope of $T_aQ$ at $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Expresses the transform subdifferential as the convex hull of actual supports generated by its active branches.
- **C-k-range** (theorem, [source](#stmt-k-range)): Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact set $\mathcal D\subset\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\mathcal D,t}<\infty$ and $K_{\mathcal D,t}<\infty$ with the following property. Let $R_0\ge2$, and let $$\varphi_0=\psi_t-t\sum_j\alpha_jw_{R_j}, \qquad 1\le R_j\le R_0, \qquad \alpha_j\ge0,$$ where the sum may be empty, finite, or countably infinite. If $a>0$, $\sum_j\alpha_j+a\le A+1$, and $R/R_0\ge K_{\mathcal D,t}$, then $$\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal D} \left\|Q_{\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\lambda)-T_aQ_{\varphi_0}(\lambda)\right\| \le C_{\mathcal D,t}\sqrt{\frac{R_0}{R}}. \label{k:range-estimate}$$ Both constants are independent of $R_0$, the number and values of the reference ranges, the reference masses, and the positive mass $a$.
  - Explanation: Approximates the ideal attraction transform by a physical finite-range attraction uniformly over earlier ranges and arbitrarily small positive masses.
- **C-k-box-pressure** (bound, [source](#stmt-k-box-pressure)): Let $\varphi_0$ and $R_0$ be as in [k:range](#k:range), allowing $\sum_j\alpha_j\le A+1$. Write $$Q^s_{\varphi_0}(\lambda) =\frac1{ts^3}\log\Xi_s^{\varphi_0}(\lambda).$$ For every compact $\mathcal K\subset\Omega$ and every $s\ge R_0$ with $s/b\in\mathbb N$, $$\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal K} \left\|Q^s_{\varphi_0}(\lambda)-Q_{\varphi_0}(\lambda)\right\| \le C_{\mathcal K,t}\frac{R_0}{s}. \label{k:box-pressure-bound}$$ More generally, for the same $s$, partition $\Lambda_{ms}$ into $m^3$ boxes of side $s$, aligned with the small-cell grid. In the potential $\varphi_0-ta w_R$, where $a\ge0$, $R\ge1$, and $\sum_j\alpha_j+a\le A+1$, delete only reference interactions between distinct boxes. If $\Xi^{\mathrm{del}}_{ms}$ is the resulting grand partition function, then $$\sup_{\lambda\in\mathcal K} \frac{\left\|\log\Xi_{ms}^{\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\lambda) -\log\Xi^{\mathrm{del}}_{ms}(\lambda)\right\|}{t(ms)^3} \le C_{\mathcal K,t}\frac{R_0}{s}. \label{k:deletion}$$ The constants have the uniformity asserted in [k:range](#k:range) and are also independent of $m,s,R$.
  - Explanation: Bounds finite-box pressure errors and deletion costs through absolute perturbation control while preserving the local core.
- **C-k-kernel-rows** (bound, [source](#stmt-k-kernel-rows)): Let $B_z$ be the box of side $s$ centered at $z$, where $z$ ranges over a translated lattice $s\mathbb Z^3$, and suppose $0<s\le R$. Define $$K^+_{zz'}=\sup_{x\in B_z,\,y\in B_{z'}}w_R(x-y), \qquad K^-_{zz'}=\inf_{x\in B_z,\,y\in B_{z'}}w_R(x-y).$$ There is a constant $C_w$ depending only on $w$ and dimension such that, with $\eta=C_ws/R$, every finite cubical collection of these boxes satisfies $$s^3\sum_{z'}K^+_{zz'}\le1+\eta. \label{k:upper-row}$$ If $z$ has distance greater than $R+4s$ from the boundary of the finite collection, then $$s^3\sum_{z'\ne z}K^-_{zz'}\ge1-\eta. \label{k:lower-row}$$ Both matrices are symmetric and nonnegative.
  - Explanation: Controls upper and lower discrete kernel row sums by the kernel integral and its global regularity.
- **C-i-bare-pressure** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-i-bare-pressure)): Let $q^{(t)}=Q_{\psi_t}$, with the pressure normalization and the core of [f:thermodynamics](#f:thermodynamics), [f:core](#f:core). Then, locally uniformly on $\Omega=(0,\infty)\times\mathbb R$, $$q^{(t)}(\beta,h)\longrightarrow q_*(\beta,h):=\max\{0,ph,4ph-9p\}. \label{i:bare-limit}$$
  - Explanation: Obtains the limiting bare-core pressure from vacuum, isolated particles, and tetrahedral clusters.
- **C-i-limiting-transform** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-i-limiting-transform)): The functions $P_1^t$ converge locally uniformly on $\Omega$ to $P_*$. For every compact $K\subset\Omega$, all their active optimizers lie in one interval $[0,U]$ for all sufficiently large $t$, and $$\sup_{\lambda\in K}\ \sup_{u\ \mathrm{active\ for}\ P_1^t(\lambda)} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(u,D_*)\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular, this holds at parameters where several limiting planes meet. If $\beta_-=\min_{(\beta,h)\in K}\beta$, the limiting objective obeys the explicit bound $$F_*(\lambda,u):=q_*(\beta,h+\beta Au)-\frac{\beta A u^2}{2} \le P_*(\lambda)-\frac{\beta_-A}{2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(u,D_*)^2 \quad(\lambda\in K,\ u\in\mathbb R). \label{i:objective-gap}$$
  - Explanation: Passes the bare-core pressure limit through the ideal transform and localizes optimizers near the limiting density set.
- **C-i-geometry** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-i-geometry)): There are constants $0<\tau<p/16$ and $\gamma>0$ with the following properties. Every choice $w_i\in\overline B(v_i,\tau)$, $i=1,2,3$, satisfies $$\overline B(g,\gamma)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{w_1,w_2,w_3\}. \label{i:robust-triangle}$$ For each distinct pair $i,j$, $$g\notin\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\bigl(\overline B(v_i,\tau)\cup\overline B(v_j,\tau)\bigr). \label{i:two-groups}$$ Moreover, for every $\lambda\in\Omega$, $$P_*(\lambda)-P_*(\lambda_*) -\left\langle g,\lambda-\lambda_*\right\rangle \ge\gamma\left\lVert\lambda-\lambda_*\right\rVert. \label{i:linear-growth}$$
  - Explanation: Makes the slope triangle robust to vertex perturbations and separates its barycenter from every two-group hull.
- **C-i-initial-separation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-i-initial-separation)): For all sufficiently large finite $t$, the size conditions in [f:stability](#f:stability), [k:transform-properties](#k:transform-properties) hold and $$\sup_{\lambda\in W_1}\left\|P_1^t(\lambda)-P_*(\lambda)\right\| <\frac{\gamma}{64}. \label{i:initial-pressure-error}$$ At every $\lambda\in W_1$ one also has the exact identity $$\partial P_1^t(\lambda) =\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\left( \partial P_1^t(\lambda)\cap \bigcup_{i=1}^3\overline B(v_i,\tau/2)\right). \label{i:initial-hull}$$ Fix one such $t$ for the rest of the construction.
  - Explanation: Fixes a finite core parameter for which actual generating supports remain near the separated limiting slope groups.
- **C-m-one-step** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-m-one-step)): Let $q$ be a pressure from the family <a href="#f:family" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="f:family">[f:family]</a>, and let $P$ be a finite convex function on $\Omega$ that is $C_*$-Lipschitz on $W_1$. Suppose $$\begin{gathered} 0<a\le A+1,\qquad 0<r<\tau,\qquad S_*a<s\le\frac14,\\ \delta\ge0,\qquad \left\lVert P-q\right\rVert_{W_1}\le\delta,\qquad \frac{2\delta}{ka}<\frac p{16}. \end{gathered}$$ Assume that throughout $W(s)$, $$\label{eq:one-step-hypothesis} \partial P(y)=\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\left(\partial P(y)\cap \bigcup_{i=1}^3\overline B(v_i,r)\right).$$ Then, with $$r^+=r+\frac{2\delta}{la}+\frac12aU_*^2,$$ one has for every $\lambda\in W(s-S_*a)$, $$\label{eq:one-step-conclusion} \partial(T_aq)(\lambda) =\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\left(\partial(T_aq)(\lambda)\cap \bigcup_{i=1}^3\overline B(v_i,r^+)\right).$$
  - Explanation: Uses the active-point parabola to isolate a density band and transfers support control across a pressure approximation error.
- **C-m-propagation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-m-propagation)): For every $n\ge1$ and $y\in W_n$, $$\label{eq:separated-generators} \partial P_n(y)= \mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\left(\partial P_n(y)\cap \bigcup_{i=1}^3\overline B(v_i,r_n)\right).$$
  - Explanation: Preserves separated generating supports at every scale on the nested parameter neighborhoods.
- **C-m-pressure-ball** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-m-pressure-ball)): For every $n\ge1$, there is $\lambda_n\in\operatorname{int}W(1/8)$ such that $$\label{eq:common-pressure-ball} \overline B(g,\gamma)\subset\partial P_n(\lambda_n).$$
  - Explanation: Finds an interior parameter at each scale whose pressure subdifferential contains the same ball of supports.
- **C-m-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-m-tail)): The function $\phi$ is bounded and continuous on $[0,\infty)$, is stable with $B=t^2/2$, and satisfies $\left\|\phi(r)\right\|\le Cr^{-3-1/32}$ for all $r\ge1$, where $$\label{eq:tail-constant} C=2^{3+1/32}t^2+t\left\lVert w\right\rVert_{\infty} \left(A R_1^{1/32} +\sum_{n\ge2}R_1^{-2^{n-1}/32}\right)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes boundedness, continuity, stability and integrable algebraic decay for the fixed infinite attraction sum.
- **C-m-canonical-corner** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-m-canonical-corner)): Suppose that $H(\beta,d)$ and $Q(\beta,h)$ have the duality and finiteness properties of [f:thermodynamics](#f:thermodynamics); assume also, as there, that $H(\,\cdot\,,d)$ is concave on $(0,\infty)$ for every $d>0$. If $$(s_1,\rho),(s_2,\rho)\in\partial Q(\beta_c,h_c), \qquad \rho>0,\quad s_1<s_2,$$ then $H(\,\cdot\,,\rho)$ has finite one-sided derivatives at $\beta_c$ and $$H'_+(\beta_c,\rho)\le-s_2<-s_1\le H'_-(\beta_c,\rho).$$
  - Explanation: Transfers pressure supports with equal density and distinct temperature coordinates to separated one-sided canonical derivatives.

          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C<\\infty$, independent of $t$ and all attraction ranges, such that for every admissible $t$, $L\\ge1$, $n\\ge0$, $\\beta>0$, and $d_L=n/L^3$, $$\\label{f:finite-bounds}\n \\beta t(cd_L^2-d_L/2)+\\frac{d_L\\log d_L-d_L}{t}\n \\le H^\\varphi_{L,n}(\\beta)\n \\le \\beta Ct d_L^2+\\frac{d_L\\log d_L}{t},$$ where $0\\log0=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-228.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-f-canonical-bounds"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-grand-tail",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$, where $\\Omega=(0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb R$ has coordinates $(\\beta,h)$. There are constants $D\\ge1$ and $a_0>0$, depending only on $t$ and $\\mathcal D$, such that, for every $\\varphi$ in <a href=\"#f:family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:family\">[f:family]</a>, $L\\ge1$, and $0\\le\\zeta\\le2(A+1)$, $$\\label{f:grand-tail-bound}\n \\sum_{n>DL^3}\\mathrm e^{thn}Z^\\varphi_{L,n}(\\beta)\n          \\exp\\left(\\frac{\\beta t\\zeta n^2}{2L^3}\\right)\n \\le\\frac{\\exp[-a_0(\\lfloor DL^3\\rfloor+1)]}{1-\\mathrm e^{-a_0}}\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D).$$ In particular the tail tends uniformly to zero, and is at most one for all sufficiently large $L$. These constants require no local occupation estimate.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-thermodynamics",
        "exact_quote": "For every member $\\varphi$ of <a href=\"#f:family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:family\">[f:family]</a>, including countably infinite sums, and every $\\beta>0$, $d\\ge0$, the limit $$\\label{f:canonical-limit}\n H_\\varphi(\\beta,d)\n =\\lim_{\\substack{L\\to\\infty\\\\ n/L^3\\to d}}\n      H^\\varphi_{L,n}(\\beta)$$ exists along every such sequence of real sides and integer counts. It is finite, convex and continuous in $d\\in[0,\\infty)$, and concave in $\\beta>0$ for each fixed $d$. The bounds <a href=\"#f:finite-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:finite-bounds\">[f:finite-bounds]</a> hold with $H_\\varphi(\\beta,d)$ and $d$ in place of their finite volume counterparts.*\n\n\n*The grand partition function and pressure satisfy $$\\label{f:pressure-definition}\n \\Xi^\\varphi_L(\\beta,h)\n =\\sum_{n\\ge0}\\mathrm e^{thn}Z^\\varphi_{L,n}(\\beta),\n \\qquad\n Q_\\varphi(\\beta,h)\n =\\lim_{L\\to\\infty}\\frac{\\log\\Xi^\\varphi_L(\\beta,h)}{tL^3}.$$ The pressure is finite, nonnegative, and jointly convex on $\\Omega$. For $\\beta>0$, $$\\label{f:duality}\n \\begin{split}\n Q_\\varphi(\\beta,h)\n   &=\\sup_{d\\ge0}\\{hd-H_\\varphi(\\beta,d)\\},\\\\\n H_\\varphi(\\beta,d)\n   &=\\sup_{h\\in\\mathbb R}\\{hd-Q_\\varphi(\\beta,h)\\},\\qquad d>0.\n \\end{split}$$ For fixed admissible $t$, pressures of the whole family are uniformly bounded on each compact subset of $\\Omega$.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },

      "exact_statement": "For every member $\\varphi$ of <a href=\"#f:family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:family\">[f:family]</a>, including countably infinite sums, and every $\\beta>0$, $d\\ge0$, the limit $$\\label{f:canonical-limit}\n H_\\varphi(\\beta,d)\n =\\lim_{\\substack{L\\to\\infty\\\\ n/L^3\\to d}}\n      H^\\varphi_{L,n}(\\beta)$$ exists along every such sequence of real sides and integer counts.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $t$ satisfying <a href=\"#f:t-requirements\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:t-requirements\">[f:t-requirements]</a> and a compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\\exp},C_1<\\infty$, depending only on $t$ and $\\mathcal D$, such that every grand canonical measure for an interaction <a href=\"#f:modified-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:modified-energy\">[f:modified-energy]</a> satisfies $$\\label{f:moment-estimates}\n \\sup_i\\mathbb E\\exp(\\beta D_0n_i^2/4)\\le C_{\\exp},\n \\qquad \\sup_{i,j}\\mathbb E[n_i n_j]\\le C_1\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D).$$ The bounds hold for every bounded region with finitely many cropped cells, and uniformly for all permitted deletions and interpolations.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-f-moments",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $t$ satisfying <a href=\"#f:t-requirements\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:t-requirements\">[f:t-requirements]</a> and a compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\\exp},C_1<\\infty$, depending only on $t$ and $\\mathcal D$, such that every grand canonical measure for an interaction <a href=\"#f:modified-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:modified-energy\">[f:modified-energy]</a> satisfies $$\\label{f:moment-estimates}\n \\sup_i\\mathbb E\\exp(\\beta D_0n_i^2/4)\\le C_{\\exp},\n \\qquad \\sup_{i,j}\\mathbb E[n_i n_j]\\le C_1\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D).$$ The bounds hold for every bounded region with finitely many cropped cells, and uniformly for all permitted deletions and interpolations.",
          "source_id": "PM-228.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $t$ satisfying <a href=\"#f:t-requirements\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:t-requirements\">[f:t-requirements]</a> and a compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$.       "id": "C-f-moments",

          "exact_quote": "Fix $t$ satisfying <a href=\"#f:t-requirements\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:t-requirements\">[f:t-requirements]</a> and a compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\\exp},C_1<\\infty$, depending only on $t$ and $\\mathcal D$, such that every grand canonical measure for an interaction <a href=\"#f:modified-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:modified-energy\">[f:modified-energy]</a> satisfies $$\\label{f:moment-estimates}\n \\sup_i\\mathbb E\\exp(\\beta D_0n_i^2/4)\\le C_{\\exp},\n \\qquad \\sup_{i,j}\\mathbb E[n_i n_j]\\le C_1\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D).$$ The bounds hold for every bounded region with finitely many cropped cells, and uniformly for all permitted deletions and interpolations.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-perturbation",
        "exact_quote": "In a fixed bounded region, let $v_\\theta=(1-\\theta)v_0+\\theta v_1$, $0\\le\\theta\\le1$, be bounded symmetric pair interactions satisfying the hypotheses of [f:moments](#f:moments) with the same $t$ and $M$. Let $J^{\\mathrm{diff}}$ be a symmetric nonnegative matrix such that $$\\left|v_1(x,y)-v_0(x,y)\\right|\\le J^{\\mathrm{diff}}_{ij}\n \\quad (x\\in C_i,\\ y\\in C_j).$$ Then, uniformly for $(\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D$, $$\\label{f:interpolation-bound}\n \\left|\\log\\Xi^{v_1}(\\beta,h)-\\log\\Xi^{v_0}(\\beta,h)\\right|\n \\le\\frac{\\beta C_1}{2}\\sum_{i,j}J^{\\mathrm{diff}}_{ij}.$$ Thus signed deletions are controlled by the absolute interaction difference, with no cancellation assumption.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-f-perturbation",
          "exact_quote": "In a fixed bounded region, let $v_\\theta=(1-\\theta)v_0+\\theta v_1$, $0\\le\\theta\\le1$, be bounded symmetric pair interactions satisfying the hypotheses of [f:moments](#f:moments) with the same $t$ and $M$. Let $J^{\\mathrm{diff}}$ be a symmetric nonnegative matrix such that $$\\left|v_1(x,y)-v_0(x,y)\\right|\\le J^{\\mathrm{diff}}_{ij}\n \\quad (x\\in C_i,\\ y\\in C_j).$$ Then, uniformly for $(\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D$, $$\\label{f:interpolation-bound}\n \\left|\\log\\Xi^{v_1}(\\beta,h)-\\log\\Xi^{v_0}(\\beta,h)\\right|\n \\le\\frac{\\beta C_1}{2}\\sum_{i,j}J^{\\mathrm{diff}}_{ij}.$$ Thus signed deletions are controlled by the absolute interaction difference, with no cancellation assumption.",
          "source_id": "PM-228.2"

      "exact_statement": "In a fixed bounded region, let $v_\\theta=(1-\\theta)v_0+\\theta v_1$, $0\\le\\theta\\le1$, be bounded symmetric pair interactions satisfying the hypotheses of [f:moments](#f:moments) with the same $t$ and $M$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-f-tail-pressure",
        "exact_quote": "For a finite or countably infinite member of <a href=\"#f:family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:family\">[f:family]</a>, let $$\\varphi_k=\\psi_t-t\\sum_{j\\le k}\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad m_k=\\sum_{j>k}\\alpha_j.$$ For every compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$, independent of the ranges, their number, and $k$, such that $$\\label{f:tail-pressure-bound}\n 0\\le Q_\\varphi(\\beta,h)-Q_{\\varphi_k}(\\beta,h)\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}m_k\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D).$$ The same upper bound holds for the difference of normalized finite volume log grand partition functions in every cube of side $L\\ge1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-f-tail-pressure",
          "exact_quote": "For a finite or countably infinite member of <a href=\"#f:family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:family\">[f:family]</a>, let $$\\varphi_k=\\psi_t-t\\sum_{j\\le k}\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad m_k=\\sum_{j>k}\\alpha_j.$$ For every compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$, independent of the ranges, their number, and $k$, such that $$\\label{f:tail-pressure-bound}\n 0\\le Q_\\varphi(\\beta,h)-Q_{\\varphi_k}(\\beta,h)\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}m_k\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D).$$ The same upper bound holds for the difference of normalized finite volume log grand partition functions in every cube of side $L\\ge1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-228.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For a finite or countably infinite member of <a href=\"#f:family\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"f:family\">[f:family]</a>, let $$\\varphi_k=\\psi_t-t\\sum_{j\\le k}\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad m_k=\\sum_{j>k}\\alpha_j.$$ For every compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$ there is a constant $C_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$, independent of the ranges, their number, and $k$, such that $$\\label{f:tail-pressure-bound}\n 0\\le Q_\\varphi(\\beta,h)-Q_{\\varphi_k}(\\beta,h)\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}m_k\n \\qquad ((\\beta,h)\\in\\mathcal D).$$ The same upper bound holds for the difference of normalized finite volume log grand partition functions in every cube of side $L\\ge1$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-k-transform-properties",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the size conditions on $t$ in [f:stability](#f:stability) hold, including $ct>4(A+1)$. For every $0<a\\le 2(A+1)$, $$T_aQ(\\beta,h)\n =\\max_{d\\ge0}\n \\left\\{hd-H(\\beta,d)+\\frac{\\beta a}{2}d^2\\right\\}.\n \\label{k:density-transform}$$ The active shifts in <a href=\"#k:transform\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"k:transform\">[k:transform]</a> are exactly the maximizing densities in <a href=\"#k:density-transform\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"k:density-transform\">[k:density-transform]</a>; in particular, they are nonnegative and form a nonempty compact set. On each compact $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$, they lie in a common interval $[0,U]$, independently of $\\varphi$ and $a$. The constant $U$ may also be chosen uniformly for all sufficiently large $t$.*\n\n\n*The function $T_aQ$ is finite and convex on $\\Omega$, and $$0\\le T_aQ-Q\\le C_{\\mathcal D}a\n \\qquad\\hbox{on }\\mathcal D.\n \\label{k:small-mass}$$ Here $C_{\\mathcal D}$ can be chosen uniformly for sufficiently large $t$. For fixed $t$, the pressures $Q$ and transforms $T_aQ$ have common bounds on compact subsets of $\\Omega$, and common local Lipschitz bounds, uniformly over the same family and masses.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-k-active-parabola",
        "exact_quote": "Let $a>0$ be as in [k:transform-properties](#k:transform-properties), and let $u$ be active at $\\lambda=(\\beta,h)$. Then for every $r\\in\\mathbb R$, $$Q(x_u+(0,r))\\le Q(x_u)+ur+\\frac{r^2}{2\\beta a}.\n \\label{k:parabola}$$ Every member of $\\partial Q(x_u)$ has second coordinate $u$.",

      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-k-active-parabola",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-k-subdifferential",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed $0<a\\le2(A+1)$ and $\\lambda=(\\beta,h)\\in\\Omega$, $$\\partial(T_aQ)(\\lambda)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\!\\bigcup_{u\\text{ active at }\\lambda}\n \\left\\{\\left(e+\\frac a2u^2,u\\right):(e,u)\\in\\partial Q(x_u)\\right\\}.\n \\label{k:support-hull}$$ The union on the right is compact, and every one of its members is an actual supporting slope of $T_aQ$ at $\\lambda$.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-k-subdifferential",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed $0<a\\le2(A+1)$ and $\\lambda=(\\beta,h)\\in\\Omega$, $$\\partial(T_aQ)(\\lambda)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\!\\bigcup_{u\\text{ active at }\\lambda}\n \\left\\{\\left(e+\\frac a2u^2,u\\right):(e,u)\\in\\partial Q(x_u)\\right\\}.\n \\label{k:support-hull}$$ The union on the right is compact, and every one of its members is an actual supporting slope of $T_aQ$ at $\\lambda$.",
          "source_id": "PM-228.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For each fixed $0<a\\le2(A+1)$ and $\\lambda=(\\beta,h)\\in\\Omega$, $$\\partial(T_aQ)(\\lambda)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\!\\bigcup_{u\\text{ active at }\\lambda}\n \\left\\{\\left(e+\\frac a2u^2,u\\right):(e,u)\\in\\partial Q(x_u)\\right\\}.\n \\label{k:support-hull}$$ The union on the right is compact, and every one of its members is an actual supporting slope of $T_aQ$ at $\\lambda$.",
      "explanation": "Expresses the transform subdifferential as the convex hull of actual supports generated by its active branches.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-k-subdifferential",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-k-range",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact set $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ and $K_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ with the following property. Let $R_0\\ge2$, and let $$\\varphi_0=\\psi_t-t\\sum_j\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad 1\\le R_j\\le R_0,\n \\qquad \\alpha_j\\ge0,$$ where the sum may be empty, finite, or countably infinite. If $a>0$, $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, and $R/R_0\\ge K_{\\mathcal D,t}$, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal D}\n \\left|Q_{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)-T_aQ_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}\\sqrt{\\frac{R_0}{R}}.\n \\label{k:range-estimate}$$ Both constants are independent of $R_0$, the number and values of the reference ranges, the reference masses, and the positive mass $a$.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-k-range",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact set $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ and $K_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ with the following property. Let $R_0\\ge2$, and let $$\\varphi_0=\\psi_t-t\\sum_j\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad 1\\le R_j\\le R_0,\n \\qquad \\alpha_j\\ge0,$$ where the sum may be empty, finite, or countably infinite. If $a>0$, $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, and $R/R_0\\ge K_{\\mathcal D,t}$, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal D}\n \\left|Q_{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)-T_aQ_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}\\sqrt{\\frac{R_0}{R}}.\n \\label{k:range-estimate}$$ Both constants are independent of $R_0$, the number and values of the reference ranges, the reference masses, and the positive mass $a$.",
          "source_id": "PM-228.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact set $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ and $K_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ with the following property. Let $R_0\\ge2$, and let $$\\varphi_0=\\psi_t-t\\sum_j\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad 1\\le R_j\\le R_0,\n \\qquad \\alpha_j\\ge0,$$ where the sum may be empty, finite, or countably infinite. If $a>0$, $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, and $R/R_0\\ge K_{\\mathcal D,t}$, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal D}\n \\left|Q_{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)-T_aQ_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}\\sqrt{\\frac{R_0}{R}}.\n \\label{k:range-estimate}$$ Both constants are independent of $R_0$, the number and values of the reference ranges, the reference masses, and the positive mass $a$.",
      "explanation": "Approximates the ideal attraction transform by a physical finite-range attraction uniformly over earlier ranges and arbitrarily small positive masses.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-k-range",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact set $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. Let $R_0\\ge2$, and let $$\\varphi_0=\\psi_t-t\\sum_j\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad 1\\le R_j\\le R_0,\n \\qquad \\alpha_j\\ge0,$$ where the sum may be empty, finite, or countably infinite. If $a>0$, $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, and $R/R_0\\ge K_{\\mathcal D,t}$, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal D}\n \\left|Q_{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)-T_aQ_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}\\sqrt{\\frac{R_0}{R}}.\n \\label{k:range-estimate}$$ Both constants are independent of $R_0$, the number and values of the reference ranges, the reference masses, and the positive mass $a$.",

      "id": "C-k-range",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-k-range",
          "exact_quote": "Fix an admissible $t$ and a compact set $\\mathcal D\\subset\\Omega$. There are constants $C_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ and $K_{\\mathcal D,t}<\\infty$ with the following property. Let $R_0\\ge2$, and let $$\\varphi_0=\\psi_t-t\\sum_j\\alpha_jw_{R_j},\n \\qquad 1\\le R_j\\le R_0,\n \\qquad \\alpha_j\\ge0,$$ where the sum may be empty, finite, or countably infinite. If $a>0$, $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, and $R/R_0\\ge K_{\\mathcal D,t}$, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal D}\n \\left|Q_{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)-T_aQ_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal D,t}\\sqrt{\\frac{R_0}{R}}.\n \\label{k:range-estimate}$$ Both constants are independent of $R_0$, the number and values of the reference ranges, the reference masses, and the positive mass $a$.",

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-k-range"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-k-box-pressure",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi_0$ and $R_0$ be as in [k:range](#k:range), allowing $\\sum_j\\alpha_j\\le A+1$. Write $$Q^s_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\n =\\frac1{ts^3}\\log\\Xi_s^{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda).$$ For every compact $\\mathcal K\\subset\\Omega$ and every $s\\ge R_0$ with $s/b\\in\\mathbb N$, $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal K}\n \\left|Q^s_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)-Q_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal K,t}\\frac{R_0}{s}.\n \\label{k:box-pressure-bound}$$ More generally, for the same $s$, partition $\\Lambda_{ms}$ into $m^3$ boxes of side $s$, aligned with the small-cell grid. In the potential $\\varphi_0-ta w_R$, where $a\\ge0$, $R\\ge1$, and $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, delete only reference interactions between distinct boxes. If $\\Xi^{\\mathrm{del}}_{ms}$ is the resulting grand partition function, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal K}\n \\frac{\\left|\\log\\Xi_{ms}^{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)\n                 -\\log\\Xi^{\\mathrm{del}}_{ms}(\\lambda)\\right|}{t(ms)^3}\n \\le C_{\\mathcal K,t}\\frac{R_0}{s}.\n \\label{k:deletion}$$ The constants have the uniformity asserted in [k:range](#k:range) and are also independent of $m,s,R$.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-k-box-pressure",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi_0$ and $R_0$ be as in [k:range](#k:range), allowing $\\sum_j\\alpha_j\\le A+1$. Write $$Q^s_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\n =\\frac1{ts^3}\\log\\Xi_s^{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda).$$ For every compact $\\mathcal K\\subset\\Omega$ and every $s\\ge R_0$ with $s/b\\in\\mathbb N$, $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal K}\n \\left|Q^s_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)-Q_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal K,t}\\frac{R_0}{s}.\n \\label{k:box-pressure-bound}$$ More generally, for the same $s$, partition $\\Lambda_{ms}$ into $m^3$ boxes of side $s$, aligned with the small-cell grid. In the potential $\\varphi_0-ta w_R$, where $a\\ge0$, $R\\ge1$, and $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, delete only reference interactions between distinct boxes. If $\\Xi^{\\mathrm{del}}_{ms}$ is the resulting grand partition function, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal K}\n \\frac{\\left|\\log\\Xi_{ms}^{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)\n                 -\\log\\Xi^{\\mathrm{del}}_{ms}(\\lambda)\\right|}{t(ms)^3}\n \\le C_{\\mathcal K,t}\\frac{R_0}{s}.\n \\label{k:deletion}$$ The constants have the uniformity asserted in [k:range](#k:range) and are also independent of $m,s,R$.",
          "source_id": "PM-228.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\varphi_0$ and $R_0$ be as in [k:range](#k:range), allowing $\\sum_j\\alpha_j\\le A+1$. Write $$Q^s_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\n =\\frac1{ts^3}\\log\\Xi_s^{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda).$$ For every compact $\\mathcal K\\subset\\Omega$ and every $s\\ge R_0$ with $s/b\\in\\mathbb N$, $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal K}\n \\left|Q^s_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)-Q_{\\varphi_0}(\\lambda)\\right|\n \\le C_{\\mathcal K,t}\\frac{R_0}{s}.\n \\label{k:box-pressure-bound}$$ More generally, for the same $s$, partition $\\Lambda_{ms}$ into $m^3$ boxes of side $s$, aligned with the small-cell grid. In the potential $\\varphi_0-ta w_R$, where $a\\ge0$, $R\\ge1$, and $\\sum_j\\alpha_j+a\\le A+1$, delete only reference interactions between distinct boxes. If $\\Xi^{\\mathrm{del}}_{ms}$ is the resulting grand partition function, then $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in\\mathcal K}\n \\frac{\\left|\\log\\Xi_{ms}^{\\varphi_0-ta w_R}(\\lambda)\n                 -\\log\\Xi^{\\mathrm{del}}_{ms}(\\lambda)\\right|}{t(ms)^3}\n \\le C_{\\mathcal K,t}\\frac{R_0}{s}.\n \\label{k:deletion}$$ The constants have the uniformity asserted in [k:range](#k:range) and are also independent of $m,s,R$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-k-kernel-rows",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B_z$ be the box of side $s$ centered at $z$, where $z$ ranges over a translated lattice $s\\mathbb Z^3$, and suppose $0<s\\le R$. Define $$K^+_{zz'}=\\sup_{x\\in B_z,\\,y\\in B_{z'}}w_R(x-y),\n \\qquad\n K^-_{zz'}=\\inf_{x\\in B_z,\\,y\\in B_{z'}}w_R(x-y).$$ There is a constant $C_w$ depending only on $w$ and dimension such that, with $\\eta=C_ws/R$, every finite cubical collection of these boxes satisfies $$s^3\\sum_{z'}K^+_{zz'}\\le1+\\eta.\n \\label{k:upper-row}$$ If $z$ has distance greater than $R+4s$ from the boundary of the finite collection, then $$s^3\\sum_{z'\\ne z}K^-_{zz'}\\ge1-\\eta.\n \\label{k:lower-row}$$ Both matrices are symmetric and nonnegative.",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-i-limiting-transform",
        "exact_quote": "The functions $P_1^t$ converge locally uniformly on $\\Omega$ to $P_*$. For every compact $K\\subset\\Omega$, all their active optimizers lie in one interval $[0,U]$ for all sufficiently large $t$, and $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in K}\\ \\sup_{u\\ \\mathrm{active\\ for}\\ P_1^t(\\lambda)}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(u,D_*)\\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular, this holds at parameters where several limiting planes meet. If $\\beta_-=\\min_{(\\beta,h)\\in K}\\beta$, the limiting objective obeys the explicit bound $$F_*(\\lambda,u):=q_*(\\beta,h+\\beta Au)-\\frac{\\beta A u^2}{2}\n \\le P_*(\\lambda)-\\frac{\\beta_-A}{2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(u,D_*)^2\n \\quad(\\lambda\\in K,\\ u\\in\\mathbb R).\n \\label{i:objective-gap}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-i-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $0<\\tau<p/16$ and $\\gamma>0$ with the following properties. Every choice $w_i\\in\\overline B(v_i,\\tau)$, $i=1,2,3$, satisfies $$\\overline B(g,\\gamma)\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\{w_1,w_2,w_3\\}.\n \\label{i:robust-triangle}$$ For each distinct pair $i,j$, $$g\\notin\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\bigl(\\overline B(v_i,\\tau)\\cup\\overline B(v_j,\\tau)\\bigr).\n \\label{i:two-groups}$$ Moreover, for every $\\lambda\\in\\Omega$, $$P_*(\\lambda)-P_*(\\lambda_*)\n       -\\left\\langle g,\\lambda-\\lambda_*\\right\\rangle\n \\ge\\gamma\\left\\lVert\\lambda-\\lambda_*\\right\\rVert.\n \\label{i:linear-growth}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-228.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-228.2"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-i-initial-separation",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large finite $t$, the size conditions in [f:stability](#f:stability), [k:transform-properties](#k:transform-properties) hold and $$\\sup_{\\lambda\\in W_1}\\left|P_1^t(\\lambda)-P_*(\\lambda)\\right|\n       <\\frac{\\gamma}{64}.\n \\label{i:initial-pressure-error}$$ At every $\\lambda\\in W_1$ one also has the exact identity $$\\partial P_1^t(\\lambda)\n =\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\left(\n       \\partial P_1^t(\\lambda)\\cap\n       \\bigcup_{i=1}^3\\overline B(v_i,\\tau/2)\\right).\n \\label{i:initial-hull}$$ Fix one such $t$ for the rest of the construction.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Stable potential** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A potential is *stable* if $U^\phi\ge-BN$ for a constant $B$ and every finite configuration.
- **Ideal attraction transform** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): For $a>0$, define $$T_aQ(\beta,h) =\sup_{u\in\mathbb R}\left\{ Q(\beta,h+\beta au)-\frac{\beta a}{2}u^2\right\}. \label{k:transform}$$
- **Active shift** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): An *active shift* at $\lambda=(\beta,h)$ is a value of $u$ attaining this supremum.
- **Shifted parameter** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): Its shifted parameter is $x_u=(\beta,h+\beta au)$.
- **Stable attraction family** ([source](#f:family)): The family of potentials used below is $$\label{f:family} \varphi(x)=\psi_t(\left\|x\right\|)-S(x),\qquad S(x)=t\sum_j\alpha_j w_{R_j}(x),\qquad R_j\ge1,\quad \alpha_j\ge0,\quad \sum_j\alpha_j\le A+1.$$
- **Mass and range schedule** ([source](#m:schedule)): For $R_1>2$, define $$\label{eq:mass-range} R_n=R_1^{2^{n-1}},\qquad a_1=A,\qquad a_n=R_n^{-1/16}\quad(n\ge2).$$
- **Final potential** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Define one potential, now with all parameters fixed, by $$\label{eq:final-potential} \Phi(x)=\phi(\left\|x\right\|) =\psi_t(\left\|x\right\|)-t\sum_{n\ge1}a_nw_{R_n}(x).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-shell-width | Shell width | = | $10^{-3}$ | The fixed relative shell width. |
| Q-shell-edges | Shell graph edge count | ≤ | $3m^2/8$ | Every finite configuration. |
| Q-cell-side | Small-cell side | = | $1/4$ | Any fixed translation of the grid. |
| Q-cell-coefficient | Quadratic cell stability coefficient | = | $t^2/8$ | Core parameter fixed and positive. |
| Q-stability | Linear stability coefficient | = | $t^2/2$ | Admissible family core parameter. |
| Q-density-coefficient | Density coercivity coefficient | = | $1/3456$ | The stated size requirements on the core parameter. |
| Q-moment-contraction | Moment recursion exponent | ≤ | $1/16$ | Common quadratic control dominates the attraction row bound. |
| Q-range-error | Physical-to-ideal pressure discrepancy | ≤ | $C_{\mathcal D,t}\sqrt{\frac{R_0}{R}}$ | Admissible references, mass budget and range-ratio threshold. |
| Q-density-center | Center of the density interval | = | $5p/3$ | The constructed fixed potential. |
| Q-critical-lower | Critical inverse temperature | ≥ | $7/8$ | The common critical point selected from the compact parameter neighborhood. |
| Q-critical-upper | Critical inverse temperature | ≤ | $9/8$ | The same common critical point. |
| Q-tail | Absolute radial potential | ≤ | $Cr^{-3-1/32}$ | The fixed complete potential for radii at least one. |
| Q-attraction-mass | Initial attraction mass | = | $3/(2p)$ | The positive packing constant. |
| Q-range-schedule | Interaction range at scale n | = | $R_1^{2^{n-1}}$ | The first range is fixed larger than two. |
| Q-mass-schedule | Attraction mass after the initial scale | = | $R_n^{-1/16}$ | Scales after the first attraction. |
| Q-realization-error | Pressure realization error bound | = | $C_3R_n^{-1/4}$ | Sufficiently large initial range; the constant is independent of the scale. |
| Q-error-mass-ratio | Pressure error divided by next attraction mass | = | $C_3R_n^{-1/8}$ | Every scale in the fixed range schedule. |
| Q-slope-energy-center | Temperature coordinate of the slope barycenter | = | $17p/4$ | Positive packing constant and initial limiting slope triangle. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-packing** [claim](#stmt-f-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-f-packing): Repeat separated maximizing configurations and match lower and upper asymptotic densities. -> Positive finite packing density.
- **R-shell** [claim](#stmt-f-shell-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-f-shell-graph): Use Gram-matrix rank obstruction and weighted graph maximization. -> Clique exclusion and edge bound.
- **R-stability** [claim](#stmt-f-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-f-stability): Apply the edge bound inside cells and the symmetric attraction row bound. -> Common quadratic stability.
- **R-limits** [claim](#stmt-f-thermodynamics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-f-thermodynamics): Use separated-box products, integer count adjustment, tail control and density supporting slopes. -> Thermodynamic limits and density-field duality.
- **R-moments** [claim](#stmt-f-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-f-moments): Close the conditional occupation estimate through a sublinear maximal-moment inequality. -> Uniform local moments and perturbation control.
- **R-transform** [claim](#stmt-k-subdifferential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-subdifferential): Use square completion, active-point parabolic control, compactness and convex separation. -> Exact active-generator support hull.
- **R-range** [claim](#stmt-k-range) / [proof](#proof-stmt-k-kernel-rows-2): Delete reference interactions between boxes and balance deletion against kernel variation; complete a square in the lower estimate. -> Uniform physical-to-ideal pressure approximation.
- **R-initial** [claim](#stmt-i-initial-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-i-initial-separation): Pass from cluster pressure asymptotics to optimizer localization and support separation. -> Separated actual support groups at a fixed finite core parameter.
- **R-persist** [claim](#stmt-m-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-propagation): Apply one-step density-band preservation with summable range and mass losses. -> Support separation at every scale.
- **R-ball** [claim](#stmt-m-pressure-ball) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-pressure-ball): Minimize the pressure minus the barycenter linear functional and force all separated groups to occur. -> A common ball of pressure supports.
- **R-final** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-m-canonical-corner-2): Pass the common support ball to the infinite-potential pressure and apply canonical slope duality. -> A shared critical inverse temperature and strict canonical derivative jump.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-weight**: For a graph with no clique of size five, combining positive weights on nonadjacent vertices can preserve total weight without decreasing the weighted edge sum, until the positive support is a clique. The weighted edge sum is at most a constant.
- **I-main**: The constructed bounded continuous stable radial pair potential has a strict downward canonical free-energy derivative jump at a common inverse temperature throughout an open interval of positive densities.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-finite**: Treat a finite graph calculation as a certificate of the canonical temperature singularity. -> The finite calculation does not establish the thermodynamic limit or the persistence of separated pressure supports.
- **A-phase**: Identify the constructed singularity with conventional fluid phases. -> The existence conclusion does not identify those phases.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite weighted graph argument supplies a weight-compression procedure. It is a local ingredient of the stability proof, with no software-domain transfer asserted.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of simple graphs with rational vertex weights.
# PRECONDITIONS: Nonnegative weights summing to one; no clique of size five; empty graph has empty weights.
# OUTPUTS: Exact compressed weights with clique support and nondecreasing weighted edge sum, or null for rejected input.
# TIME: O(n^5+n^3) rational operations per graph.
# SPACE: O(n^2) adjacency storage plus rational weights.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact python-flint rational arithmetic; bit cost depends on input denominators.
# FAILURE: Invalid input returns null; malformed JSON raises an error.
import json, sys
from itertools import combinations
from flint import fmpq

def compress(case):
    n=case['n']
    if type(n) is not int or n<0: return None
    raw=case['edges']
    if any(len(e)!=2 or any(type(v) is not int or v<0 or v>=n for v in e) or e[0]>=e[1] for e in raw): return None
    edges={tuple(e) for e in raw}
    if len(edges)!=len(raw): return None
    weights=[fmpq(x) for x in case['weights']]
    if len(weights)!=n or any(x<0 for x in weights) or sum(weights)!=int(n>0): return None
    if any(all((i,j) in edges for i,j in combinations(c,2)) for c in combinations(range(n),5)): return None
    adj=[set() for _ in range(n)]
    for i,j in edges: adj[i].add(j); adj[j].add(i)
    for _ in range(n):
        pair=next(((i,j) for i,j in combinations(range(n),2) if weights[i]>0 and weights[j]>0 and j not in adj[i]),None)
        if pair is None: break
        i,j=pair
        si=sum((weights[k] for k in adj[i]),fmpq(0))
        sj=sum((weights[k] for k in adj[j]),fmpq(0))
        keep,drop=(i,j) if si>=sj else (j,i)
        weights[keep] += weights[drop]
        weights[drop] = fmpq(0)
    return [str(x) for x in weights]

print(json.dumps([compress(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

    "output_contract": "Compressed rational weights whose positive support is a clique, conserving total weight and not decreasing the weighted edge sum; null marks a rejected input.",
    "reason": "The finite weighted graph argument supplies a weight-compression procedure. It is a local ingredient of the stability proof, with no software-domain transfer asserted.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of simple graphs with rational vertex weights.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Nonnegative weights summing to one; no clique of size five; empty graph has empty weights.\n# OUTPUTS: Exact compressed weights with clique support and nondecreasing weighted edge sum, or null for rejected input.\n# TIME: O(n^5+n^3) rational operations per graph.\n# SPACE: O(n^2) adjacency storage plus rational weights.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact python-flint rational arithmetic; bit cost depends on input denominators.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input returns null; malformed JSON raises an error.\nimport json, sys\nfrom itertools import combinations\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndef compress(case):\n    n=case['n']\n    if type(n) is not int or n<0: return None\n    raw=case['edges']\n    if any(len(e)!=2 or any(type(v) is not int or v<0 or v>=n for v in e) or e[0]>=e[1] for e in raw): return None\n    edges={tuple(e) for e in raw}\n    if len(edges)!=len(raw): return None\n    weights=[fmpq(x) for x in case['weights']]\n    if len(weights)!=n or any(x<0 for x in weights) or sum(weights)!=int(n>0): return None\n    if any(all((i,j) in edges for i,j in combinations(c,2)) for c in combinations(range(n),5)): return None\n    adj=[set() for _ in range(n)]\n    for i,j in edges: adj[i].add(j); adj[j].add(i)\n    for _ in range(n):\n        pair=next(((i,j) for i,j in combinations(range(n),2) if weights[i]>0 and weights[j]>0 and j not in adj[i]),None)\n        if pair is None: break\n        i,j=pair\n        si=sum((weights[k] for k in adj[i]),fmpq(0))\n        sj=sum((weights[k] for k in adj[j]),fmpq(0))\n        keep,drop=(i,j) if si>=sj else (j,i)\n        weights[keep] += weights[drop]\n        weights[drop] = fmpq(0)\n    return [str(x) for x in weights]\n\nprint(json.dumps([compress(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)]))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "valid_compression",

This supplies an inferred finite graph simplification contract; the continuum existence theorem supplies no general computational solver. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-model** (hypothesis): The construction concerns identical classical particles with radial pair interactions in Euclidean space. No engineering transfer contract is established. Blocked use: ['Using thermodynamic stability as a software resource, protocol correctness, or latency guarantee.']
- **L-phases** (counterexample_scope): The singularity is an existence result for the constructed infinite-range interaction; its phases are not identified as conventional fluid phases. Blocked use: ['Labeling the resulting coexistence as identified liquid and vapor phases.']
- **L-core** (hypothesis): Common stability and occupation bounds require the stated size conditions on the fixed construction parameter. Blocked use: ['Applying the family estimates without checking the repulsion and attraction margins.']
- **L-limit** (approximation): Canonical existence is a thermodynamic limit statement, including countably infinite attraction sums. Finite-volume observations alone do not establish the derivative jump. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite particle ensemble as a certificate of the limiting singularity.']
- **L-convex** (approximation): Uniform convergence of convex pressures alone does not preserve a corner; the proof additionally controls separated supporting slopes. Blocked use: ['Inferring persistence of nondifferentiability solely from uniform pressure approximation.']
- **L-mass** (edge_case): Compact active-shift arguments concern positive attraction mass. The degenerate zero-mass shift representation does not have the asserted compact active set. Blocked use: ['Extending active-set compactness to the degenerate shift representation.']
- **L-range** (hypothesis): The physical-to-ideal range estimate requires an admissible fixed core parameter, a compact parameter set, bounded reference ranges, the total mass budget, and the range-ratio threshold. Blocked use: ['Using the range estimate outside its stated reference and added-attraction hypotheses.']
- **L-choice** (nonconstructive): The construction fixes a sufficiently large finite core parameter through qualitative convergence and subsequently enlarges the initial range to meet summability budgets. An explicit numerical parameter instance and critical-parameter evaluation procedure are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Presenting the existence construction as a certified numerical generator of the critical inverse temperature.']
- **L-finite** (formal_scope): The finite weighted-graph argument concentrates nonnegative normalized weights onto a clique without decreasing the edge objective. It does not by itself certify the continuum geometric obstruction or the thermodynamic singularity. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite graph implementation as a certificate of the entire continuum theorem.']
- **L-dual** (hypothesis): The passage from pressure supports to canonical slopes requires the stated duality and finiteness properties, concavity in inverse temperature, positive density, and distinct supporting slopes at a common parameter. Blocked use: ['Applying the canonical derivative-jump implication without its duality and support hypotheses.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 228 (`lean/docs/228.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A continuum temperature singularity for a radial pair potential](../../preprints/A-continuum-temperature-singularity-for-a-radial-pair-potential-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [A radial continuum phase transition with algebraic decay](../../preprints/A-radial-continuum-phase-transition-with-algebraic-decay-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization constructs one stable radial pair potential in three-dimensional continuum space with a divergent repulsive core and an integrable tail that is $o(r^{-3})$. Its canonical free energy exists for every positive inverse temperature and density. At one inverse temperature $\beta_c\in(1/2,3/2)$, the free energy has a strict finite jump between its one-sided derivatives with respect to inverse temperature, for every density in one nonempty open interval.
>
> The formalization constructs a bounded continuous stable radial pair potential in $\mathbb R^3$ satisfying $|\phi(r)|\le Cr^{-3-1/32}$ for $r\ge1$. There is a nonempty open interval of positive densities around $5p/3$, where $p$ is the unit-separated packing-density limit, on which the canonical free energy exists at every positive inverse temperature and has a strict downward derivative jump at one common $\beta_c\in[7/8,9/8]$.
>
> The earlier fixed-density statement at $5p/3$ is also retained. Both statements concern the derivative with respect to inverse temperature.

Comparator source `RadialTransition.lean` (2306 bytes, sha256 `305e7a1b2a0cb643b5bc8667dca0e2f138b64c3b375b3f79ffe169103dc7e8ad`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators NNReal
open Filter MeasureTheory Set

namespace RadialTransition

abbrev Position := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 3)
abbrev Potential := ℝ≥0 → ℝ

def cube (L : ℝ) : Set Position :=
  {x | ∀ k : Fin 3, 0 ≤ x k ∧ x k ≤ L}

def configurationCube (L : ℝ) (N : ℕ) : Set (Fin N → Position) :=
  {x | ∀ i : Fin N, x i ∈ cube L}

def energy (φ : Potential) {N : ℕ} (x : Fin N → Position) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i : Fin N, ∑ j : Fin N with i < j, φ (nndist (x i) (x j))

def partition (φ : Potential) (L : ℝ) (N : ℕ) (β : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (N.factorial : ℝ)⁻¹ *
    ∫ x in configurationCube L N, Real.exp (-β * energy φ x)

def packingNumber (L : ℝ) : ℕ :=
  sSup {n : ℕ | ∃ X : Finset Position, X.card = n ∧
    (∀ x ∈ X, x ∈ cube L) ∧
    (∀ x ∈ X, ∀ y ∈ X, x ≠ y → 1 ≤ dist x y)}

def IsPackingDensity (p : ℝ) : Prop :=
  Tendsto (fun L : ℝ => (packingNumber L : ℝ) / L ^ 3) atTop (nhds p)

def IsBoundedPotential (φ : Potential) : Prop :=
  ∃ M : ℝ, 0 ≤ M ∧ ∀ r : ℝ≥0, |φ r| ≤ M

def StableWith (φ : Potential) (B : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (N : ℕ) (x : Fin N → Position), -(B * N) ≤ energy φ x

def AlgebraicDecayWith (φ : Potential) (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ r : ℝ≥0, 1 ≤ r → |φ r| ≤ C * (r : ℝ) ^ (-(3 : ℝ) - 1 / 32)

def IsCanonicalFreeEnergy (φ : Potential) (ρ : ℝ) (f : ℝ → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ β : ℝ, 0 < β →
    Tendsto (fun L : ℝ =>
      -(Real.log (partition φ L ⌊ρ * L ^ 3⌋₊ β) / (β * L ^ 3)))
      atTop (nhds (f β))

def StrictTemperatureCorner (f : ℝ → ℝ) (βc : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ dLeft dRight : ℝ,
    HasDerivWithinAt f dLeft (Iic βc) βc ∧
    HasDerivWithinAt f dRight (Ici βc) βc ∧
    dRight < dLeft ∧ ¬DifferentiableAt ℝ f βc

theorem main :
    ∃ (p : ℝ) (φ : Potential) (B C : ℝ) (f : ℝ → ℝ) (βc : ℝ),
      0 < p ∧ IsPackingDensity p ∧
      IsBoundedPotential φ ∧ Continuous φ ∧
      0 < B ∧ 0 < C ∧ StableWith φ B ∧ AlgebraicDecayWith φ C ∧
      IsCanonicalFreeEnergy φ (5*p/3) f ∧
      βc ∈ Icc (7/8:ℝ) (9/8) ∧ StrictTemperatureCorner f βc := by
  sorry

end RadialTransition

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a bounded, continuous, stable radial pair potential in three dimensions with $`|\phi(r)|\le Cr^{-3-1/32}`$ for <i>r</i> ≥ 1. Throughout an open interval of positive densities, its canonical thermodynamic free energy is finite at every positive inverse temperature and has a strict downward derivative jump at one common finite positive inverse temperature.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A phase transition is a singularity that appears in the thermodynamic limit of a particle system. We construct one for a radial pair interaction with a bounded continuous core and an explicit integrable power-law bound on its tail.

We consider identical classical particles in three-dimensional Euclidean space. For a radial pair potential $\phi$, a cube $\Lambda_L=[0,L]^3$, and $N\ge0$, set <a id="eq:partition"></a>$$\label{eq:partition}
 U^\phi(x_1,\ldots,x_N)=\sum_{1\le i<j\le N}\phi(\left|x_i-x_j\right|),
 \qquad
 Z^\phi_{L,N}(\beta)=\frac1{N!}
  \int_{\Lambda_L^N}\exp(-\beta U^\phi)\,\,\mathrm dx_1\cdots\,\mathrm dx_N,$$ with $Z^\phi_{L,0}=1$. The position measure is fixed independently of the inverse temperature $\beta>0$. A potential is *stable* if $U^\phi\ge-BN$ for a constant $B$ and every finite configuration.

Let $p$ denote the packing constant $$p=\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{M_L}{L^3},
 \qquad
 M_L=\max\bigl\{\#X:X\subset[0,L]^3,\
               \left|x-y\right|\ge1\ \text{for distinct }x,y\in X\bigr\}.$$ The existence and positivity of $p$ are proved in [f:packing](#f:packing); its numerical value is not needed.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There is a bounded continuous function $\phi:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb R$ and finite constants $B,C>0$ such that $$\sum_{i<j}\phi(\left|x_i-x_j\right|)\ge-BN,
 \qquad
 \left|\phi(r)\right|\le C r^{-3-1/32}\quad(r\ge1).$$ There are an open interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$ centered at $5p/3$ and a common inverse temperature $\beta_c\in[7/8,9/8]$ such that, for every $\rho\in I$, the canonical free energy $$f(\beta,\rho)=
 -\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac1{\beta L^3}
       \log Z^\phi_{L,\lfloor\rho L^3\rfloor}(\beta)$$ exists and is finite for every $\beta>0$. Its finite one-sided derivatives at $\beta_c$ satisfy $$f'_-(\beta_c,\rho)>f'_+(\beta_c,\rho).$$ In particular, $f(\,\cdot\,,\rho)$ is not differentiable at $\beta_c$.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and the role of the tail

The existence of continuum phase transitions belongs to the program highlighted by Simon’s problems in mathematical physics (Simon 1984) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Simon1984](#ref-Simon1984)]. We address the explicit interaction class and canonical singularity in [thm:main](#thm:main).

Rigorous continuum coexistence results precede the present construction. Ruelle proved a transition for a symmetric two-species classical system (Ruelle 1971) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Ruelle1971](#ref-Ruelle1971)]. Lebowitz, Mazel, and Presutti established a liquid–vapor transition for a continuum model with an attractive pair term and a repulsive four-body term (Joel L. Lebowitz, Mazel, and Presutti 1998, 1999) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LebowitzMazelPresutti1998](#ref-LebowitzMazelPresutti1998), [LebowitzMazelPresutti1999](#ref-LebowitzMazelPresutti1999)]. Dereudre and Renaud-Chan establish density coexistence for a class of saturated continuum interactions (Dereudre and Renaud-Chan 2026, Theorems 1–2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[DereudreRenaudChan2026](#ref-DereudreRenaudChan2026)]. Their radial-kernel example integrates over the union of particle neighborhoods, giving a many-body energy (Dereudre and Renaud-Chan 2026, sec. 2.3, Example 3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DereudreRenaudChan2026](#ref-DereudreRenaudChan2026)]. The species or many-body structures are part of these models; the problem considered here uses a single species and a sum of radial pair interactions.

Long-range weak attractions provide a classical route from microscopic particle interactions to a nonconvex mean-field free energy. The Lebowitz–Penrose theory identifies the limiting free energy through convexification of a reference free energy with a quadratic attraction term (J. L. Lebowitz and Penrose 1966) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[LebowitzPenrose1966](#ref-LebowitzPenrose1966)]. Gerardi, Marchioro, Olivieri, and Presutti extended this framework from hard-core references to superstable interactions (Gerardi et al. 1973) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GerardiEtAl1973](#ref-GerardiEtAl1973)]. In the present problem, the limit of an interaction as its range tends to infinity is only an intermediate object: the required singularity must belong to one fixed pair potential. Moreover, a pointwise power bound constrains how the masses of its successive attractions may depend on their ranges.

The companion article (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[OpenAICompanion2026](#ref-OpenAICompanion2026)] constructs a radial potential with a divergent repulsive core, an integrable $o(r^{-3})$ tail, and a common transition inverse temperature for an open interval of densities. Both articles use small clusters and separated supports of a pressure in two variables. Here we prove the bounded-core construction and the quantitative range estimate needed for a fixed power margin. All proofs required for the present theorem are included below. The two articles concern different potentials, each with its own density interval and common critical inverse temperature.

A recent theorem of He, Jauslin, Lebowitz, and Peled proves a liquid–vapor transition for a finite-range modified Kac pair interaction (He et al. 2026, Equation (1.13), Theorems 1.3 and 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[HeJauslinLebowitzPeled2026](#ref-HeJauslinLebowitzPeled2026)]. Their interaction is defined using a fixed partition into boxes, and the coexistence theorem is formulated at a fixed inverse temperature. Our physical interaction is a radial function of particle separation; boxes enter only the estimates used to analyze its integrals. The conclusion here is a strict inverse-temperature derivative jump of the canonical free energy at one common critical point throughout an open interval of densities. It is an existence result for the constructed infinite-range interaction, with no identification of its phases as conventional fluid phases.

The estimates also have several classical ancestors. The quadratic occupation control is part of the superstability framework developed by Ruelle (Ruelle 1970) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Ruelle1970](#ref-Ruelle1970)]. For our particular core, the needed bound follows from the weighted graph maximization argument of Motzkin and Straus (Motzkin and Straus 1965, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[MotzkinStraus1965](#ref-MotzkinStraus1965)]; we reproduce the argument and its geometric application. Supporting slopes and partial convex duality are used in their standard finite-dimensional sense (Rockafellar and Wets 1998, Theorem 11.1 and Proposition 11.3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[RockafellarWets1998](#ref-RockafellarWets1998)]. The local occupation estimates, the quantitative attraction comparison, and the persistence argument are proved at their precise strengths in this paper.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

There are three tasks: construct a stable family of bounded potentials, compare a long-range attraction with its ideal density transform, and preserve a sufficiently large set of pressure supports while the attraction ranges grow. We describe how their outputs fit together.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### A bounded reference family.

A parameter $t>1$ fixes the microscopic core; it is chosen once and is independent of $\beta$. Below distance one, the core has height $t^2$ outside a narrow shell at distances comparable to $\mathrm e^{-t}$; inside the shell it has a zero-energy plateau. It vanishes beyond a fixed radius. Four particles can form a tetrahedron with all six pair distances in the zero plateau, whereas five cannot lie in the shell. This gives a quadratic lower bound in the particle counts of small cubes. The bound survives the addition of nonnegative radial attractions with a prescribed total mass. [f:section](#f:section), [f:local-section](#f:local-section) use it to establish the canonical and grand-canonical limits, uniform occupation moments, and continuity of pressure under a small change of attraction mass.

For a potential $\varphi$ in this family and a density $d\ge0$, write $$H_\varphi(\beta,d)
 =-\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{1}{tL^3}
        \log Z^\varphi_{L,\lfloor dL^3\rfloor}(\beta),
 \qquad
 Q_\varphi(\beta,h)
 =\sup_{d\ge0}\{hd-H_\varphi(\beta,d)\}.$$ Thus $H_\varphi=\beta f_\varphi/t$, and $h$ is the field dual to density. The pressure $Q_\varphi$ is convex jointly in $(\beta,h)$. A supporting slope $(e,d)$ at $(\beta_0,h_0)$ means that $$Q_\varphi(\beta,h)\ge Q_\varphi(\beta_0,h_0)
             +e(\beta-\beta_0)+d(h-h_0)$$ for every $\beta>0$ and $h\in\mathbb R$. Two such slopes with the same density $d=\rho$ and distinct first coordinates give two distinct upper supporting slopes of $H_\varphi(\,\cdot\,,\rho)$. This is the route to the derivative jump, proved explicitly in [m:canonical-corner](#m:canonical-corner).

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### An attraction with controlled error.

<a id="eq:intro-transform"></a>
For a pressure $Q$, an ideal attraction of mass $a>0$ acts by $$\label{eq:intro-transform}
 (T_aQ)(\beta,h)
 =\sup_{u\in\mathbb R}
   \left\{Q(\beta,h+\beta au)-\frac{\beta a}{2}u^2\right\}.$$ Square completion identifies its maximizing shifts $u$ with maximizing densities after addition of the quadratic attraction term. [k:section-transform](#k:section-transform) proves attainment, uniform bounds on these shifts, and an exact formula for all supporting slopes of $T_aQ$.

Fix a nonnegative radial $C^1$ kernel $w$, supported in the unit ball and of integral one, and put $w_R(x)=R^{-3}w(x/R)$. If the reference interaction $\varphi_0$ has range at most $R_0$, then [k:range](#k:range) compares the physical attraction $-ta w_R$ with the ideal transform: $$\bigl\|Q_{\varphi_0-ta w_R}-T_aQ_{\varphi_0}\bigr\|_{\mathcal D}
 \le C_{\mathcal D,t}\sqrt{R_0/R}$$ on each compact parameter set $\mathcal D$. The constant is uniform over earlier reference interactions and over positive masses $a$ tending to zero. Boxes of side comparable to $\sqrt{R_0R}$ balance the cost of deleting reference interactions across boxes against the variation of the new kernel inside them. For the lower bound, independent reference grand ensembles are placed in these boxes. Completing a square avoids any need to identify their mean density with the maximizing shift, so the comparison remains valid at coexistence without selecting a phase.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Three slope groups and one fixed potential.

As $t$ grows, the bare-core pressure tends to $\max\{0,ph,4ph-9p\}$. Its branches come from vacuum, single particles, and four-particle clusters, respectively. A first ideal attraction turns these into three affine branches with noncollinear slopes in the $(e,d)$ plane. [i:section](#i:section) proves that, for one large finite $t$, the actual generating supports remain near these three slopes throughout a parameter neighborhood.

Uniform convergence of convex pressures by itself need not preserve a corner. The additional control comes from the following property of a maximizing shift in <a href="#eq:intro-transform" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-transform">[eq:intro-transform]</a>: at its shifted parameter $(\beta,h+\beta au)$, the reference pressure has a parabolic upper bound in the field variable (). In [m:one-step](#m:one-step) this bound confines nearby supports to one density band and hence to one of the separated slope groups. An error $\delta$ in pressure then costs $O(\delta/a)$ in the slopes.

After the first attraction, take $R_{n+1}=R_n^2$ and $a_n=R_n^{-1/16}$. The range errors are $O(R_n^{-1/4})$, and their ratios to the next masses are $O(R_n^{-1/8})$. All relevant losses are summable. [sec:iteration](#sec:iteration) consequently preserves a common two-dimensional ball of pressure supports, passes it to the limit, and uses horizontal slices near density $5p/3$ to obtain the canonical singularity at a common inverse temperature throughout a density interval. The same schedule gives $\sum_n a_nR_n^{1/32}<\infty$, which supplies the pointwise tail bound. The core, all attraction parameters, and the density interval are fixed before the limiting critical parameter is selected.

<a id="f:section"></a>
### A stable family and its thermodynamic functions

We first construct a bounded repulsive core which permits clusters of at most four particles at very short separations. Its quadratic control of local particle numbers will be uniform over all attraction ranges used later. Throughout the proof, all position integrals use Lebesgue measure in one fixed microscopic length unit, and all finite volumes have free boundaries.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Packing and the microscopic core

<a id="stmt-f-packing"></a>
<div id="f:packing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 1** (Packing density). *Let $M_L$ be the maximum cardinality of a subset of $[0,L]^3$ whose distinct points have mutual distances at least one. Then $$p=\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{M_L}{L^3}$$ exists and belongs to $(0,\infty)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-f-packing"><summary>Proof of stmt-f-packing</summary>

*Proof.* Disjoint balls of radius $1/3$ about the points lie in $[-1/3,L+1/3]^3$, so $M_L\le C(L+1)^3$. This also bounds the possible cardinalities; compactness of the configuration space for each cardinality shows that the maximum is attained. The integer lattice gives a positive lower asymptotic density.

Fix $s>0$ and repeat a maximizing configuration in $[0,s]^3$ by translations in $(s+1)\mathbb Z^3$. Points in distinct translates are separated by at least one: in a coordinate in which the translates differ, their coordinate separation is at least $(s+1)-s=1$. Taking all complete translates contained in $[0,L]^3$ gives $$\liminf_{L\to\infty}\frac{M_L}{L^3}
 \ge \frac{M_s}{(s+1)^3}.$$ Letting $s\to\infty$ along a sequence realizing the upper asymptotic density makes the right side tend to that upper density. The lower and upper limits therefore agree. ◻

</details>

Set $A=3/(2p)$ and $\Delta=10^{-3}$. The parameter $t>1$ below is a construction parameter, distinct from inverse temperature $\beta$. We shall eventually choose one sufficiently large $t$ and keep it fixed. Put $$l_t=\mathrm e^{-t},\qquad I_t=[l_t,(1+\Delta)l_t],
 \qquad 2(1+\Delta)l_t<1.$$ Define $\psi_t:[0,\infty)\to[0,t^2]$ by <a id="f:core"></a>$$\label{f:core}
 \begin{aligned}
 \psi_t(r)&=t^2
   &&\text{on }[0,l_t]\cup[(1+\Delta)l_t,1],\\
 \psi_t(r)&=0
   &&\text{on }[(1+\Delta/3)l_t,(1+2\Delta/3)l_t]
                    \cup[1+1/t,\infty),
 \end{aligned}$$ and make it affine on each of the three intervening intervals. Thus $\psi_t$ is continuous, including at zero, nonnegative, and supported in $[0,2]$.

<a id="f:family"></a>
Fix a radial nonnegative $C^1$ function $w$ on $\mathbb R^3$, supported in the closed unit ball, with $\int_{\mathbb R^3}w(x)\,\,\mathrm dx=1$. For example, one may normalize $((1-\left|x\right|^2)_+)^2$. Write $w_R(x)=R^{-3}w(x/R)$.
